# TASKS.md — Evidence Ledger

**Last audited: 2026-09-26 on `release-v0.1.1-closeout`. The prior completion claim was invalid: S83.157, S83.158, S83.163, and the terminal S83.166 release action remain open, and S91.1 is reopened for the ownership/concurrency repair. Checked-task status contradictions now fail closed in the ledger validator, active-work inventory, and release readiness. No v0.1.1 promotion is permitted until each open predecessor has independent completion evidence, the exact candidate passes local and hosted gates, and the published artifacts pass deployment and rollback verification.**

S83.179 phase-two follow-up: report exact prior pass receipts through a bounded shadow replay-eligibility audit without skipping tests. `scripts/ci_batch_replay_audit.py` snapshots only the prior exact-SHA generation, then the serial runner executes Pytest, preserves fresh branch coverage and node outcomes, and finishes owned cleanup before comparison. It refuses candidate, tree, dependency, command, environment, platform, declared-input, node-ID, outcome, and semantic-coverage drift plus dirty/restricted inputs, corruption, ambiguity, unsafe paths, malformed identities, and partial/unbounded indexes; caps the audit at 512 candidates/16 MiB of manifests inside the existing two-generation/2 GiB store; emits content-free `skips=0` markers; and provides an independent audit-off rollback. Receipt admission, coverage restoration, reconciliation, and attestation composition remain later reviewed phases. Failing-first collection proved the missing module; runner tests then proved the absent auditor session/rollback option; a later failing-first malformed-plan case proved invalid command identity initially degraded to a miss. Repaired phase-two tests are 39/39 green and the six-file focused runner slice is 268/268 green. Branch-aware Coverage.py evidence records 89% aggregate across `ci_batch_receipts.py` at 89%, `ci_batch_replay_audit.py` at 92%, and `run_ci_shards_serial.py` at 88%, with no measured file below 75%. Scoped Ruff, strict mypy, Markdown lint, task integrity, and task-ledger validation are green; commit and exact-head full-gate evidence remain intentionally pending while the canonical gate owns the repository.

S83.179 observability follow-up: `scripts/ci_gate_progress.py` now emits one canonical, machine-readable `GATE-PROGRESS-SHADOW` snapshot after each executed batch, derived only from the known canonical plan and strictly validated completed receipts. It distinguishes executed/pass/fail from prior-receipt candidate/missing/ineligible counts, labels ETA as an estimate, and keeps `gate_result=unknown`, `overall_green=null`, `terminal_phases_complete=false`, and `skips=0` even when all planned batches pass. Malformed, mixed-candidate, foreign-plan, ambiguous, unsafe, mismatched-execution, or unbounded timing evidence invalidates the entire ETA basis. The plan and snapshot cap at 512 batches, retained durations at 64, accepted durations at 24 hours, output at 4 KiB, and storage remains inside the existing two-generation/2 GiB boundary; no paths, tests, nodes, logs, environment values, secrets, or pickle enter output. `--no-shadow-gate-progress` rolls back only this renderer, while audit-off mode performs no historical receipt reads. Failing-first collection proved the missing module, and a later red case proved that a new receipt was initially accepted for the wrong executed coordinate. Repaired progress tests are 34/34 green, the replay-ordering suite is 39/39 green, and branch-aware Coverage.py reports 92% for `ci_gate_progress.py`, above both the 85% aggregate and 75% per-file floors. Scoped Ruff and strict mypy, Markdown lint, task integrity, and task-ledger validation are green; admission, coverage restoration, terminal reconciliation, full-gate proof, commit, merge, and push remain out of scope while the canonical gate owns the repository.

S83.179 non-reusable failure-receipt follow-up: `scripts/ci_batch_receipts.py` and the serial runner now atomically publish a bounded diagnostic only after an executed nonzero batch has exact expected/observed identity, successful owned cleanup, and disk reserve. The content address binds candidate and batch coordinates, stable failure class, elapsed time, and at most 32 hashed failing-node records; raw node names, filenames, arbitrary paths, XML text, exception bodies, captured output, environment values, secrets, payloads, and pickle are excluded. The replay auditor treats every exact or related failure receipt as `prior-failure-non-reusable`, including coexistence with pass evidence; progress surfaces `failure_receipts` while retaining `gate_result=unknown`, `overall_green=null`, and `skips=0`, and failure timing is excluded from ETA evidence. Storage shares the two-generation/2 GiB ceiling, caps failures at 256 per generation and all indexed receipts at 512/16 MiB, removes partial staging trees, and uses `--no-shadow-failure-receipts` for narrow rollback without changing one-worker/16-file execution, application traffic, or release evidence. Failing-first collection proved the missing API, and a later red coexistence case proved a pass could initially hide prior failure evidence. Repaired failure tests are 41/41 green; the eight-file serial slice is 343/343 green; branch-aware Coverage.py is 88% aggregate with `ci_batch_receipts.py` 88%, `ci_batch_replay_audit.py` 85%, `ci_gate_progress.py` 92%, and `run_ci_shards_serial.py` 88%, with no file below 75%. Commit, merge, push, full-gate proof, and receipt admission remain intentionally pending while the canonical gate owns the repository.

S83.179 receipt-authentication follow-up: `scripts/ci_receipt_auth.py` reuses the existing gate-attestation 32-byte local key lifecycle and standard-library HMAC-SHA256 model with a receipt-specific domain, canonical JSON, constant-time comparison, a seven-day default/30-day maximum validity window, at most eight captured trust keys, and at most 32 revocations. Pass and sanitized failure writers atomically publish private envelopes that bind receipt kind, action digest, and exact manifest digest without storing key bytes, key paths, receipt paths, test content, exception bodies, environment values, secrets, or pickle. Replay and progress distinguish `verified`, `unsigned-legacy`, `unknown-signer`, `tampered`, `revoked`, `expired`, and `malformed`; only a verified pass is labelled future-eligible, verified failures remain non-reusable, ETA rejects every nonverified sample, and `skip_authorized=false`/`skips=0` remain fixed. Verification is deterministic, offline, and bounded inside the existing 512-candidate/16 MiB index and two-generation/2 GiB store; it adds no workers, daemons, listeners, services, or batch width. `--no-shadow-receipt-authentication` provides a narrow unsigned-legacy rollback without changing execution. Failing-first runner evidence proved the missing authentication marker; repaired authentication tests are 23/23 green and the nine-file serial slice is 366/366 green. Branch-aware Coverage.py is 88% aggregate with `ci_receipt_auth.py` 98%, `ci_batch_receipts.py` 88%, `ci_batch_replay_audit.py` 85%, `ci_gate_progress.py` 92%, and `run_ci_shards_serial.py` 88%, with no measured file below 75%. The feature document records Pants cache-poisoning/rate-limit, cosign trust-expiry, and Bazel remote-cache credential reports and explains why local authentication remains shadow-only; commit, merge, push, full-gate proof, hosted provenance, and receipt admission remain intentionally pending while the canonical gate owns the repository.

It reuses the existing confined-log, canonical-JSON/SHA-256, and stable failure-class primitives; revalidates source identity around the read; rejects traversal, symlinks, unavailable/mutating sources, invalid UTF-8, truncation, mixed runs or terminals, malformed/duplicate/ambiguous markers, count/result drift, and limit breaches; and caps input at 64 MiB/250,000 lines/1 MiB per line, failure receipts at 64, and JSON output at 32 KiB. S83.180 Git-native prediction continuation: each rehearsal group now runs a
deterministic, injected-runner matrix of target/source and source/source
`git merge-tree --write-tree --name-only --no-messages -z` probes. The additive
result is `predicted-clean`, `conflicted`, or fail-closed `blocked`; it contains no
mergeability claim, and unsupported Git, ambiguous output, timeouts, output/path/
pair overflow, and terminal freshness drift all block admission while discarding
partial details. The predictor caps groups at 64 checks, each command at ten
seconds and 262,144 characters per stream, and conflict paths at 10,000 scanned/

`KeyError: 'conflict_prediction'`; repaired focused coverage passes 125/125 at 92%
aggregate, with `branch_reconciliation_inventory.py` at 91% and

records capability-based Git compatibility, the exact admission sequence,
pairwise-versus-cumulative limitations, bounded GitLab #28424/#383730 practitioner

and the exact-head full gate remain pending while the canonical gate owns shared

S83.180 no-branch-left-behind snapshot continuation: every terminal plan now
seals the target plus every originally observed ref/tip/classification into a
sorted `snapshot_basis`, including ancestor and patch-equivalent branches outside
the candidate queue. The opt-in `--reconciliation-snapshot` CLI consumes that
plan, its exact basis digest, a fresh exhaustive inventory and exact canonical
digest, plus bounded exact ref/tip retirements; it emits deterministic branch and
head states (`pending`, `merged`, `stale`, `blocked`, or `explicitly-retired`),
complete counts, and a new snapshot digest without invoking Git. Missing,
duplicate, unknown, malformed, truncated, target-identity-mismatched, or
digest-mismatched evidence fails with one content-free error. The failing-first
test reproduced a missing `canonical_document_digest` API; repaired focused tests
pass 128/128. The first coverage repair attempt honestly failed at 82.9% branch
coverage; adversarial schema/digest/accounting tests raised final coverage to 90%
aggregate (`branch_reconciliation_inventory.py` 91%, plan types 90%) with both
the aggregate branch floor and per-file 75% floors passing. Scoped Ruff, strict
mypy, and the 10,144-file line policy pass. Documentation records bounded
resource/privacy behavior, ZDD/rollback, and long-lived GitLab #229156/#1089 lost
state and moving-inventory reports. Broad collection, commit, and the exact-head
full gate remain pending while the canonical gate owns shared test capacity.

S83.180 authenticated retirement continuation: `explicitly-retired` can no longer
turn a missing ref into success from an unverified ref/tip assertion. Each exact
planned identity now requires either fresh ancestry/patch-equivalence for the same
head, or a bounded version-2 Ed25519 operator approval binding the plan digest,
branch, head, fixed reason code, reviewer-identity digest, key ID, and issue/expiry
times. Verification reuses `general_ludd.self_update.signing.verify_signature`
and the locked `cryptography` dependency against an explicit sorted public-key
store with active/revoked state; no new cryptography was invented. Unsigned legacy

duplicates, stale heads, oversized evidence, free-form reasons, and verifier
failure return the content-free snapshot error. Output records only bounded proof
metadata and trust digest, never reviewer identity. The failing-first signed
fixture reproduced `InventoryError: invalid reconciliation snapshot` against the
legacy digest parser. The repaired focused file passes 130/130; targeted coverage
passes at 90% aggregate with both production files at 90%, above the 85% aggregate

GitHub #120203 practitioner boundaries. Broad collection, commit, and an exact-head
full gate remain pending while the canonical gate owns shared test capacity.

Azure operational knowledge, cross-backend task proof, and guarded promotion.

general capabilities.

  S91.1 gate-deadlock evidence: the first full replay passed 3,398 integration
  tests and all pre-test phases, then reproduced the circular clean-tree
  rejection before unit shards. Four failing-first regressions now prove that
  dirty preflight is content-stable and non-release, release-policy mode remains
  clean-only, and the canonical gate selects the preflight mode. The complete
  shard-runner/observability/attestation neighborhood passes 113/113; a new full
  replay remains required.

  The next full replay reached `unit-1b:batch-015` before exposing a stale
  structural assertion that still required spending one of ten historical
  dispatch slots on release orchestration. The corrected contract keeps release
  orchestration on the main thread under the zero-to-three policy and passes
  22/22 focused tests; another complete replay remains required.

  The following full replay reached `unit-1b:batch-024` before exposing a stale
  floor-v2 assertion that still required a ten-agent default. Repairing that
  assertion prompted a wider automated policy scan, which found an active
  exactly-ten dispatch checker, wave documentation, two operational skills,
  the operational-discipline specification, and state/plugin E2E fixtures that
  still encoded unreachable or make-work ownership. Failing-first dispatch
  diversity coverage reproduced 6 failures before the checker was bounded to
  one-to-three voluntary prompts. The focused wave suite passes 17/17 and the
  expanded runtime/state/policy replay passes 225/225. The structural guard now
  scans every active OpenCode skill and Claude agent prompt so this drift cannot
  hide behind a checked task again; another complete replay remains required.

  Markdown validation of the newly active skill inventory exposed a live-state
  reload target that still restored a mandatory floor of ten. Its regression
  failed first, then passed after `reload-enforcement` adopted the canonical
  zero default, validated numeric input, clamped the operator ceiling to three,
  and clamped the floor to that ceiling. The target-contract checker passes;
  behavioral replay proved `FLOOR=99`/`CEILING=99` materializes `3`, then the
  canonical invocation restores `0` with enforcement re-armed. The expanded
  affected replay passes 249/249, scoped Ruff and mypy are green, locked
  Markdown lint reports zero issues, Makefile validation reports 1,075 targets
  with zero duplicates, and task registration covers all 91 changed paths.

  The next full gate passed every pre-test phase and all 3,398 integration tests,
  then `unit-1b:batch-026` exposed three runtime cases that still expected a
  mandatory floor while exercising the new zero-default contract. The repaired
  tests opt into a positive floor explicitly, and the floor plugin now accepts

  consume or mutate live session state. The complete affected runtime/PID/floor
  replay passes 247/247 with one intentional skip; hook-runtime passes 149/149
  with 18 intentional skips, OpenCode E2E passes 44/44, Node v26 compatibility
  passes 5/5, the plugin manifest passes 105/105, and scoped Ruff is green. The
  next exact-candidate replay cleared the former `unit-1b:batch-026`

  `tests/unit/test_env_var_audit_deep.py` passes 25/25 and markdown lint passes.
  The release-documentation replay then exposed two more stale ownership
  assertions in `test_pipeline_priority.py`: “at least 5 of 10” and an obsolete
  ten-slot anti-pattern phrase. They now pin the canonical at-least-two-of-three
  allocation; the combined release-verifier/documentation/pipeline suite passes
  175/175 and the additional cross-document mirror regression passes 1/1. A
  Active-policy follow-up: the failing-first cross-policy checker found that
  `AGENTS.md`, `docs/specs/BEHAVIORAL_SPECS.md`, and the active
  background-test-runner skill could still demand session-start waves,
